## **0\. Contract Position**

PREDECESSORS:  
  STEP-141..149  
  STEP-115..140

PRIMARY\_OBJECT:  
  Cross-Certificate Lifecycle Integrity

SECONDARY\_OBJECTS:  
  Currentness Certification Closure  
  Revocation Residual Obligation Ledger  
  Cross-Certificate Dependency Reconciliation  
  Lifecycle Reference Integrity Audit  
  Residual Obligation Ownership and Closure

AUTHORITY:  
  HISTORY \= CANONICAL TRUTH  
  STATE \= fold(HISTORY)  
  PROJECTION \= DERIVED  
  LLM \= OBSERVATION / PROPOSAL ONLY  
  KERNEL \= SOLE CANONICAL COMMIT AUTHORITY

EXECUTION\_MODE:  
  DESIGN\_ONLY  
  NO\_REPOSITORY\_INSPECTION  
  NO\_IMPLEMENTATION  
  NO\_TEST\_EXECUTION  
  NO\_CANONICAL\_MUTATION

STEP-150 defines the contract for proving that a collection of related currentness certificates, invalidation and revocation events, consumer states, and residual obligations is internally consistent and that its claimed closure is supported by authoritative evidence.

STEP-149 governs the lifecycle of an individual currentness certificate and its associated regression, revocation, and re-certification processes. STEP-150 governs cross-certificate integrity and the closure of obligations that span multiple certificate lifecycles.

The central requirement is:

> A certificate lifecycle MUST NOT be declared globally closed merely because one certificate has been revoked, one replacement certificate has been issued, or one consumer cohort has converged. Closure requires reconciliation of the complete in-scope dependency set and explicit disposition of every mandatory residual obligation.

## **1\. Purpose**

1. Define the cross-certificate dependency model and its authoritative snapshot.  
2. Establish a residual obligation ledger for revocation, invalidation, supersession, remediation, and re-certification.  
3. Detect orphaned, contradictory, stale, duplicated, or unreachable lifecycle references.  
4. Reconcile historical certificates with currently operative certificates.  
5. Establish deterministic closure criteria across multiple related certificate lifecycles.  
6. Prevent a replacement certificate from concealing unresolved obligations inherited from prior certificates.  
7. Define ownership, evidence, and authority requirements for residual obligation closure.  
8. Detect inconsistencies between certificate registries, canonical History, consumer state, and downstream authorization records.  
9. Preserve unknown and conflicted states instead of treating missing records as successful closure.  
10. Maintain the separation between lifecycle integrity certification, operational re-entry authorization, and Freeze.

## **2\. Scope and Non-Goals**

### **2.1 In scope**

* Cross-certificate identity and dependency relationships.  
* Versioned certificate dependency snapshots.  
* Historical and operative certificate classification.  
* Residual obligation creation, assignment, transfer, closure, and reopening.  
* Revocation and invalidation obligation reconciliation.  
* Cross-certificate conflict and reference-integrity detection.  
* Closure completeness and evidence sufficiency.  
* Lifecycle integrity audit results.  
* Canonical commit and projection reconciliation.  
* Audit reproducibility and deterministic replay.

### **2.2 Non-goals**

STEP-150 does not independently:

* issue a Stability Certificate or currentness certificate;  
* perform the original stability or currentness assessment;  
* revoke or invalidate certificates without the required authority;  
* execute corrective actions or external operations;  
* decide every cross-contract policy conflict;  
* assume that all historical certificates must remain operative;  
* delete historical certificates after closure;  
* authorize operational re-entry;  
* authorize production deployment;  
* authorize normative adoption or Freeze.

Any action requiring an authority or mechanism not established by predecessor contracts MUST remain blocked or explicitly unresolved.

## **3\. Normative State Distinctions**

CERTIFICATE\_ISSUED \!= CERTIFICATE\_REGISTERED  
CERTIFICATE\_REGISTERED \!= CERTIFICATE\_OPERATIVE  
CERTIFICATE\_OPERATIVE \!= CERTIFICATE\_CURRENT  
CERTIFICATE\_CURRENT \!= CROSS\_CERTIFICATE\_CONSISTENT

CERTIFICATE\_SUPERSEDED \!= HISTORICAL\_RECORD\_DELETED  
CERTIFICATE\_REVOKED \!= ALL\_DEPENDENT\_CERTIFICATES\_RECONCILED  
CERTIFICATE\_REPLACED \!= PRIOR\_OBLIGATIONS\_CLOSED  
OBLIGATION\_CREATED \!= OBLIGATION\_ASSIGNED  
OBLIGATION\_ASSIGNED \!= OBLIGATION\_EXECUTED  
OBLIGATION\_EXECUTED \!= OBLIGATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
OBLIGATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED \!= OBLIGATION\_CLOSED

OBLIGATION\_CLOSED \!= ALL\_OBLIGATIONS\_CLOSED  
ALL\_DISCOVERED\_OBLIGATIONS\_CLOSED \!= OBLIGATION\_SET\_COMPLETE  
OBLIGATION\_SET\_COMPLETE \!= CLOSURE\_CERTIFIED  
CLOSURE\_CERTIFIED \!= OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED

REGISTRY\_CONSISTENT \!= HISTORY\_CONSISTENT  
PROJECTION\_CONSISTENT \!= CANONICAL\_STATE\_VALID  
NO\_REFERENCES\_DISCOVERED \!= NO\_REFERENCES\_EXIST  
NO\_OPEN\_FINDINGS \!= AUDIT\_COMPLETE  
AUDIT\_PASSED \!= NORMATIVE\_ADOPTION

COMMIT\_REQUESTED \!= COMMIT\_COMPLETED  
COMMIT\_ACK\_MISSING \!= COMMIT\_FAILED  
REPLAY \!= REEXECUTION  
REPLAY \!= CANONICAL\_MUTATION

OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZED

These distinctions MUST be reflected in the authoritative schema, lifecycle state machine, validation rules, and test oracles. Implementations MUST NOT collapse them into a single Boolean if doing so can conceal unresolved state.

## **4\. Cross-Certificate Integrity Model**

### **4.1 Graph model**

The system MUST represent relevant certificate lifecycles and obligations as a versioned dependency graph or an equivalent structure with demonstrable completeness and integrity.

Required node classes:

STABILITY\_CERTIFICATE  
CURRENTNESS\_CERTIFICATE  
CERTIFICATE\_REVISION  
CURRENTNESS\_ASSESSMENT  
COHORT\_SNAPSHOT  
BASELINE  
POLICY\_REVISION  
DEPENDENCY\_SNAPSHOT  
CONSUMER\_ACCEPTANCE  
INVALIDATION\_EVENT  
REVOCATION\_EVENT  
SUPERSESSION\_EVENT  
RECERTIFICATION\_RECORD  
DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION  
EXECUTION\_PERMIT  
OPERATION\_RECORD  
SAFE\_STATE\_ENFORCEMENT  
RESIDUAL\_OBLIGATION  
CLOSURE\_ASSESSMENT  
LIFECYCLE\_INTEGRITY\_CERTIFICATE

Required relationship classes:

DERIVED\_FROM  
CERTIFIES  
SUPERSEDES  
INVALIDATES  
REVOKES  
DEPENDS\_ON  
ACCEPTED\_BY  
CONSUMED\_BY  
TRIGGERS  
REQUIRES  
ASSIGNED\_TO  
DISCHARGED\_BY  
VERIFIED\_BY  
REOPENS  
CLOSED\_BY

The authoritative vocabulary MUST be reconciled with the existing contract corpus before adoption. This list is a proposed minimum, not a claim that every relation already exists in the repository.

### **4.2 Relationship semantics**

Every relationship MUST have:

* a source identity and revision;  
* a target identity and revision;  
* a defined relationship type;  
* provenance or event reference;  
* an applicable scope;  
* an effective lifecycle interval or state condition, where relevant;  
* validation status;  
* evidence reference;  
* authoritative ordering information.

A graph edge MUST NOT be accepted solely because an LLM proposed it or because a projection contains it. Its acceptance MUST follow the applicable validation and canonical commit rules.

### **4.3 Snapshot binding**

Each cross-certificate audit MUST bind to a snapshot that identifies:

cross\_certificate\_snapshot:  
  snapshot\_id: REQUIRED  
  snapshot\_revision: REQUIRED  
  snapshot\_digest: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_history\_position: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_registry\_revision: REQUIRED  
  lifecycle\_event\_ledger\_revision: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_graph\_revision: REQUIRED  
  residual\_obligation\_ledger\_revision: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_state\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_state\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  policy\_set\_digest: REQUIRED  
  scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  capture\_time: REQUIRED  
  completeness\_status: REQUIRED

`capture_time` MUST NOT substitute for the canonical History position or revision identifiers. Clock time alone cannot establish a consistent distributed snapshot.

### **4.4 Snapshot validity**

A snapshot is eligible for audit only when the required source revisions and their integrity evidence can be established.

Permitted snapshot statuses:

VALID  
INCOMPLETE  
STALE  
CONFLICTED  
UNKNOWN  
INVALID

An `INCOMPLETE`, `STALE`, `CONFLICTED`, `UNKNOWN`, or `INVALID` snapshot MUST NOT support an unqualified lifecycle-integrity closure claim.

The audit MAY return a partial result, but it MUST identify the exact scope and claims that remain unverified.

## **5\. Certificate Classification and Operative-State Resolution**

### **5.1 Classification dimensions**

The audit MUST distinguish at least two independent dimensions.

Historical lifecycle classification

DRAFT  
REGISTERED  
ISSUED  
PUBLISHED  
ACCEPTED  
SUPERSEDED  
INVALIDATED  
REVOKED  
EXPIRED  
RETIRED  
UNKNOWN

Operative-use classification

OPERATIVE  
OPERATIVE\_WITH\_CONSTRAINTS  
NON\_OPERATIVE  
USE\_PROHIBITED  
REVALIDATION\_REQUIRED  
CONFLICTED  
UNKNOWN

These vocabularies MUST be aligned with STEP-148 and STEP-149. They are not interchangeable: a historical certificate can remain permanently recorded while no longer being eligible for operative use.

### **5.2 Operative certificate resolution**

For each in-scope certificate and scope, the audit MUST establish:

1. The certificate's canonical identity and revision.  
2. Its current lifecycle status in authoritative History.  
3. Any committed revocation, invalidation, supersession, or expiry conditions.  
4. The replacement relationship, if one exists.  
5. Whether the replacement is valid for the same scope and intended use.  
6. Whether consumers and downstream authorizations have reconciled the transition.  
7. Whether residual obligations remain attached to the old or new certificate.  
8. Whether conflicting operative references exist.

The audit MUST NOT assume that the highest numeric revision is operative without applying the authoritative revision and supersession rules.

### **5.3 Multiple operative references**

If multiple certificates appear operative for overlapping scopes, the system MUST evaluate whether concurrent operation is explicitly permitted.

If permission cannot be established, the result MUST be `CONFLICTED` or `UNKNOWN`, and the relevant gate MUST apply the governing restrictive policy.

A later timestamp, greater revision number, or more recent publication alone MUST NOT resolve the conflict.

### **5.4 Historical continuity**

Supersession and revocation MUST preserve historical continuity. The system MUST be able to reconstruct which certificate revision supported a prior decision and whether that decision was valid under the rules and evidence applicable at that time.

Current invalidity does not, by itself, prove that a historical decision was invalid when made. Historical adjudication requires its own evidence and policy.

## **6\. Residual Obligation Ledger**

### **6.1 Definition**

A residual obligation is an unresolved requirement arising from a certificate lifecycle, invalidation, revocation, supersession, regression, remediation, or audit finding that must be completed, verified, accepted as an explicitly authorized exception, or otherwise disposed of under a governing contract.

The residual obligation ledger is the authoritative inventory of those obligations within its declared scope. Its projection MUST be reconstructible from canonical History.

### **6.2 Obligation creation triggers**

An obligation MUST be considered for creation when a validated event establishes or potentially establishes a requirement for follow-up.

Examples include:

* certificate revocation with incomplete propagation;  
* invalidation of a certificate consumed by downstream authorization;  
* a required consumer that has not reconciled a replacement;  
* an operation whose outcome remains unknown;  
* a corrective action that has executed but lacks effect verification;  
* a stale certificate reference discovered after prior closure;  
* an incomplete dependency scan;  
* conflicting certificate lifecycle records;  
* a failed closure assessment;  
* a policy amendment requiring reassessment;  
* a trust-key revocation affecting prior acceptance evidence;  
* an authorized exception approaching expiry.

The obligation-creation policy MUST define which events create mandatory obligations and which create provisional findings awaiting adjudication.

### **6.3 Obligation record**

residual\_obligation:  
  obligation\_id: REQUIRED  
  obligation\_revision: REQUIRED  
  obligation\_type: REQUIRED  
  lifecycle\_status: REQUIRED  
  severity: REQUIRED

  source\_event\_refs: REQUIRED  
  source\_certificate\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  affected\_consumer\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_authorization\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_operation\_refs: REQUIRED

  requirement\_ref: REQUIRED  
  governing\_policy\_ref: REQUIRED  
  owner\_identity: REQUIRED  
  responsible\_authority: REQUIRED

  creation\_commit\_ref: REQUIRED  
  creation\_time: REQUIRED  
  due\_condition: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_obligation\_refs: REQUIRED

  execution\_evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  effect\_verification\_refs: REQUIRED  
  closure\_decision\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  exception\_ref: OPTIONAL

  last\_verified\_state\_ref: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_findings: REQUIRED

An optional field MUST remain absent until the referenced event or artifact actually exists. The schema MUST NOT fabricate a closure decision, exception, or commit reference.

### **6.4 Obligation identity**

Obligation identity MUST be stable across retries and state projections. A revision MUST identify a changed record without erasing the prior record.

The authoritative identity policy MUST define how to handle:

* repeated detection of the same underlying issue;  
* separate obligations caused by one event;  
* multiple events affecting one obligation;  
* obligations transferred between owners;  
* obligations reopened after closure;  
* duplicate records discovered during reconciliation.

Duplicate detection MUST NOT merge obligations when doing so would lose distinct scope, ownership, authorization, or evidence requirements.

### **6.5 Obligation states**

DETECTED  
VALIDATION\_PENDING  
VALIDATED  
OPEN  
ASSIGNMENT\_PENDING  
ASSIGNED  
BLOCKED  
EXECUTION\_PENDING  
EXECUTED  
EFFECT\_VERIFICATION\_PENDING  
EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
CLOSURE\_PENDING  
CLOSED  
EXCEPTION\_PENDING  
EXCEPTION\_AUTHORIZED  
REOPENED  
SUPERSEDED  
CANCELLED  
UNKNOWN  
CONFLICTED

`CANCELLED` MUST NOT be used as a substitute for closing an obligation whose required action remains outstanding. Cancellation requires an authorized determination that the obligation is no longer applicable, with provenance and impact evidence.

`SUPERSEDED` MUST identify the replacement obligation or decision and preserve any unfinished requirements that were not actually discharged.

## **7\. Obligation Ownership, Transfer, and Closure**

### **7.1 Ownership requirements**

Every validated mandatory obligation MUST have a responsible owner or an explicit unassigned status with a corresponding assignment obligation.

Ownership means responsibility for coordinating and evidencing resolution. It does not automatically grant authority to approve the action, accept residual risk, or commit canonical state.

The contract MUST distinguish:

OWNER\_ASSIGNED \!= ACTION\_AUTHORIZED  
ACTION\_AUTHORIZED \!= ACTION\_EXECUTED  
ACTION\_EXECUTED \!= EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
EFFECT\_VERIFIED \!= OBLIGATION\_CLOSED

### **7.2 Ownership transfer**

An ownership transfer MUST be an auditable lifecycle event containing:

* the obligation identity and revision;  
* previous and new owner identities;  
* transfer authority;  
* acceptance or acknowledgment evidence required by policy;  
* outstanding dependencies and deadlines;  
* current execution and verification status;  
* canonical commit reference.

A transfer MUST NOT reset the obligation's age, erase escalation history, or convert a blocked obligation to an unblocked state without evidence.

### **7.3 Closure predicates**

An obligation may be closed only when its applicable closure predicate is satisfied.

OBLIGATION\_CLOSABLE :=  
    OBLIGATION\_IDENTITY\_VALID  
AND REQUIREMENT\_DISPOSITION\_ESTABLISHED  
AND REQUIRED\_ACTIONS\_COMPLETE  
AND REQUIRED\_EFFECTS\_VERIFIED  
AND REQUIRED\_EVIDENCE\_VALID  
AND DEPENDENCY\_OBLIGATIONS\_DISPOSED  
AND NO\_BLOCKING\_CONFLICT  
AND CLOSURE\_AUTHORITY\_VALID

The governing policy MUST specify which predicates apply to each obligation type. Any non-applicable predicate requires an explicit, authorized classification; missing data is not evidence of non-applicability.

### **7.4 Exceptions**

If an obligation cannot be fully discharged, an exception MAY be considered only when the governing policy permits it.

An exception MUST identify:

* the exact obligation and scope;  
* the reason for non-closure;  
* the approving authority;  
* the residual risk and affected dependencies;  
* compensating controls;  
* effective conditions and expiry;  
* required monitoring and reassessment;  
* revocation or reopening triggers.

An exception is a separate authorized disposition, not proof that the underlying technical action occurred.

The audit MUST report `CLOSED_WITH_AUTHORIZED_EXCEPTION` or an equivalent distinct result when permitted. It MUST NOT report unqualified technical closure.

### **7.5 Reopening**

A previously closed obligation MUST be reopened or linked to a new obligation when evidence establishes that:

* the closure evidence was invalid or revoked;  
* the required effect has regressed;  
* a dependent reference has reappeared;  
* the obligation's scope was incomplete;  
* a closure predicate was not actually satisfied;  
* a governing policy requires renewed verification.

The original closure decision MUST remain in History. Reopening MUST add a new auditable transition.

## **8\. Cross-Certificate Dependency Reconciliation**

### **8.1 Reconciliation targets**

The audit MUST reconcile the following relationships, to the extent they are in scope:

1. Stability Certificate → currentness certificate.  
2. Currentness certificate → source assessment.  
3. Currentness certificate → cohort and dependency snapshots.  
4. Certificate revision → supersession and revocation events.  
5. Revocation event → affected consumers.  
6. Consumer → accepted certificate revision.  
7. Certificate → downstream authorization and permit.  
8. Authorization → operation and external effect.  
9. Regression → corrective action and re-certification.  
10. Lifecycle event → residual obligation.  
11. Residual obligation → owner, evidence, and closure decision.  
12. Closure decision → supporting integrity audit.

### **8.2 Reconciliation dimensions**

Each relationship MUST be assessed for:

IDENTITY\_MATCH  
REVISION\_MATCH  
DIGEST\_MATCH  
SCOPE\_COMPATIBILITY  
TEMPORAL\_OR\_CAUSAL\_ORDER\_VALID  
LIFECYCLE\_STATUS\_COMPATIBLE  
AUTHORITY\_VALID  
EVIDENCE\_COMPLETE  
DEPENDENCY\_STATE\_CURRENT

The precise checks depend on relationship type. The adopted schema MUST define which dimensions are mandatory for each edge class.

### **8.3 Reconciliation outcomes**

CONSISTENT  
CONSISTENT\_WITH\_CONSTRAINTS  
MISSING\_REFERENCE  
ORPHANED\_REFERENCE  
REVISION\_MISMATCH  
DIGEST\_MISMATCH  
SCOPE\_CONFLICT  
ORDERING\_CONFLICT  
LIFECYCLE\_CONFLICT  
AUTHORITY\_UNVERIFIED  
EVIDENCE\_INCOMPLETE  
DEPENDENCY\_STALE  
UNKNOWN

Each non-success result MUST identify the affected relationship, relevant source snapshot, evidence, and required follow-up.

### **8.4 Orphaned references**

A reference is orphaned when its target cannot be resolved under the authoritative identity and retention rules, or when the relationship is invalid under the applicable lifecycle contract.

The audit MUST distinguish:

* genuinely missing target;  
* target not yet replicated or published;  
* target retired under an authorized lifecycle;  
* target unavailable to the current audit;  
* target identity collision;  
* target with invalid or conflicting provenance.

The audit MUST NOT automatically delete an orphaned reference. It MUST create a finding or obligation and preserve the evidence needed for adjudication.

### **8.5 Contradictory references**

If two authoritative records make incompatible claims about the same identity, revision, scope, or lifecycle transition, the system MUST record a conflict.

The conflict-resolution procedure MUST be explicit and authority-based. Arrival order, lexical ordering, model confidence, or an arbitrary preference for the latest projection MUST NOT serve as an implicit adjudication rule.

## **9\. Lifecycle Integrity Audit**

### **9.1 Audit inputs**

A lifecycle integrity audit MUST bind to:

* the cross-certificate snapshot;  
* the certificate registry and lifecycle ledger revisions;  
* the dependency graph revision;  
* the residual obligation ledger revision;  
* the relevant consumer and authorization state snapshots;  
* applicable contract and policy revisions;  
* the audit rule set and validator revision;  
* the declared audit scope and cutoff;  
* the evidence manifest.

### **9.2 Audit stages**

INPUT\_VALIDATION  
  \-\> SNAPSHOT\_INTEGRITY\_VALIDATION  
  \-\> CERTIFICATE\_IDENTITY\_RESOLUTION  
  \-\> OPERATIVE\_STATE\_RESOLUTION  
  \-\> DEPENDENCY\_GRAPH\_VALIDATION  
  \-\> REFERENCE\_INTEGRITY\_CHECK  
  \-\> REVOCATION\_AND\_SUPERSESSION\_RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> RESIDUAL\_OBLIGATION\_RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> DOWNSTREAM\_DEPENDENCY\_RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> EVIDENCE\_SUFFICIENCY\_CHECK  
  \-\> CLOSURE\_PREDICATE\_EVALUATION  
  \-\> AUDIT\_RESULT\_COMMIT, IF AUTHORIZED

The implementation MAY partition or parallelize stages if it preserves snapshot consistency, deterministic decision semantics, and traceable evidence.

### **9.3 Audit outcomes**

PASS  
PASS\_WITH\_AUTHORIZED\_CONSTRAINTS  
FAIL  
INCOMPLETE  
CONFLICTED  
UNKNOWN  
INVALID\_INPUT  
AUDIT\_ERROR

`PASS` requires every mandatory audit predicate to be satisfied within the declared scope and snapshot.

`PASS_WITH_AUTHORIZED_CONSTRAINTS` is permitted only if an adopted policy explicitly defines the result and its restrictions. It MUST NOT be treated as equivalent to unrestricted closure.

`INCOMPLETE`, `CONFLICTED`, `UNKNOWN`, `INVALID_INPUT`, and `AUDIT_ERROR` MUST NOT be projected to `PASS`.

### **9.4 Audit result**

lifecycle\_integrity\_audit:  
  audit\_id: REQUIRED  
  audit\_revision: REQUIRED  
  audit\_rule\_set\_id: REQUIRED  
  validator\_revision: REQUIRED

  scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  policy\_set\_digest: REQUIRED

  certificate\_count: REQUIRED  
  relationship\_count: REQUIRED  
  obligation\_count: REQUIRED

  findings: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_obligation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_dependency\_refs: REQUIRED  
  conflict\_refs: REQUIRED

  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED  
  outcome: REQUIRED  
  assessor\_identity: REQUIRED  
  decision\_authority: REQUIRED

  canonical\_commit\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  audit\_timestamp: REQUIRED

Counts are descriptive metadata. A zero count MUST NOT establish completeness unless the discovery process and snapshot are independently validated.

### **9.5 Read-only guarantee**

The audit phase MUST be read-only with respect to source certificates, lifecycle events, consumer state, authorization records, and obligation state.

If the audit produces a proposed repair, assignment, reopening, or closure action, that proposal MUST be separated from audit observation and submitted through the applicable authorized workflow.

The audit MUST NOT silently repair the graph, infer missing edges as canonical, close obligations, revoke certificates, or mutate the canonical registry.

## **10\. Global Closure Certification**

### **10.1 Closure subject**

A closure certification MUST identify exactly what is being declared closed:

* a single certificate lifecycle;  
* a set of related certificate lifecycles;  
* a revocation and its downstream obligations;  
* a re-certification cycle;  
* a defined dependency subgraph;  
* a residual obligation set;  
* a broader cross-certificate integrity scope.

The certification MUST NOT claim closure beyond its declared scope.

### **10.2 Closure completeness predicate**

CROSS\_CERTIFICATE\_CLOSURE\_ELIGIBLE :=  
    SNAPSHOT\_VALID  
AND AUDIT\_SCOPE\_VALID  
AND CERTIFICATE\_IDENTITIES\_RESOLVED  
AND OPERATIVE\_CERTIFICATE\_SET\_RESOLVED  
AND REQUIRED\_DEPENDENCIES\_COMPLETE  
AND REQUIRED\_LIFECYCLE\_TRANSITIONS\_RECONCILED  
AND REQUIRED\_REVOCATION\_EFFECTS\_VERIFIED  
AND REQUIRED\_CONSUMER\_STATES\_RECONCILED  
AND REQUIRED\_AUTHORIZATION\_STATES\_RECONCILED  
AND REQUIRED\_OPERATION\_EFFECTS\_RECONCILED  
AND RESIDUAL\_OBLIGATION\_SET\_COMPLETE  
AND ALL\_MANDATORY\_OBLIGATIONS\_DISPOSED  
AND ALL\_REQUIRED\_EXCEPTIONS\_AUTHORIZED  
AND EVIDENCE\_MANIFEST\_VALID  
AND NO\_BLOCKING\_CONFLICT  
AND NO\_UNRESOLVED\_MANDATORY\_FINDING

This predicate is a proposed normative model. The adopted contract MUST define the applicable predicates and their evidence requirements. No predicate may be treated as satisfied merely because its record is absent.

### **10.3 Closure outcomes**

NOT\_ELIGIBLE  
ELIGIBILITY\_PENDING  
ELIGIBLE  
AUTHORIZATION\_PENDING  
COMMIT\_PENDING  
CLOSURE\_COMMITTED  
CLOSURE\_VERIFIED  
CLOSED\_WITH\_AUTHORIZED\_CONSTRAINTS  
REOPENED  
BLOCKED  
UNKNOWN

`CLOSURE_COMMITTED` means the authorized closure decision was recorded in canonical History.

`CLOSURE_VERIFIED` means the required post-commit verification established that the committed decision and resulting state satisfy the closure contract.

These states MUST remain separate.

### **10.4 Closure certificate**

cross\_certificate\_closure\_certificate:  
  closure\_certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  closure\_certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  closure\_certificate\_digest: REQUIRED

  audit\_id: REQUIRED  
  audit\_revision: REQUIRED  
  audit\_digest: REQUIRED

  scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_set\_digest: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_graph\_digest: REQUIRED  
  obligation\_ledger\_digest: REQUIRED

  operative\_certificate\_refs: REQUIRED  
  historical\_certificate\_refs: REQUIRED  
  revocation\_event\_refs: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_obligation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  authorized\_exception\_refs: REQUIRED

  closure\_policy\_ref: REQUIRED  
  closure\_predicate\_results: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED

  issuer\_identity: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_ref: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_commit\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  verification\_result\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  lifecycle\_status: REQUIRED

The `canonical_commit_ref` and `verification_result_ref` MUST remain absent until those events have actually occurred.

### **10.5 No implicit closure**

The following conditions MUST block unqualified closure:

* incomplete dependency discovery;  
* unknown operative certificate state;  
* required consumer state not reconciled;  
* mandatory revocation effect not verified;  
* mandatory residual obligation still open;  
* expired or invalid evidence needed by a closure predicate;  
* unresolved lifecycle conflict;  
* unauthorized exception;  
* missing authorization or commit evidence;  
* snapshot drift that invalidates the audit basis.

Where a policy permits partial closure, the certificate MUST identify the exact closed and unresolved portions. Partial closure MUST NOT be represented as global closure.

## **11\. Post-Closure Integrity and Reopening**

### **11.1 Closure validity is conditional**

A closure certificate proves only the predicates, scope, evidence, and snapshot to which it is bound. It MUST NOT be interpreted as an eternal guarantee that all related certificate lifecycles remain consistent.

The governing policy MUST define the events that trigger revalidation, invalidation, or reopening.

### **11.2 Reopening triggers**

Potential triggers include:

NEW\_DEPENDENCY\_DISCOVERED  
CERTIFICATE\_REVOCATION  
CERTIFICATE\_SUPERSESSION  
CONSUMER\_STATE\_REGRESSION  
AUTHORIZATION\_STATE\_REGRESSION  
OPERATION\_OUTCOME\_RESOLVED\_DIFFERENTLY  
EVIDENCE\_INVALIDATION  
TRUST\_KEY\_REVOCATION  
POLICY\_CHANGE  
SCOPE\_CHANGE  
BASELINE\_CHANGE  
OBLIGATION\_CLOSURE\_INVALIDATED  
ORPHANED\_REFERENCE\_REAPPEARED  
CLOSURE\_SNAPSHOT\_INVALIDATED

The mapping from trigger to required response MUST be explicit. A new event MUST NOT automatically reopen every historical closure if the event is demonstrably outside its scope; equally, scope exclusion MUST be evidence-backed.

### **11.3 Reopening procedure**

When a reopening trigger is validated:

1. Identify the affected closure certificate and its exact scope.  
2. Determine which closure predicates may no longer hold.  
3. Create or link the relevant finding and residual obligations.  
4. Evaluate dependent certificates, authorizations, and operations.  
5. Apply required restrictions under separate authorization.  
6. Commit a reopening decision if required by policy.  
7. Re-run the applicable audit against a new valid snapshot.  
8. Issue a new closure decision only after the required predicates pass.

The prior closure record MUST remain available. Reopening MUST NOT retroactively rewrite the earlier audit result.

### **11.4 Recurrence**

If a previously resolved inconsistency recurs, the system MUST preserve the relationship between the prior finding, closure decision, recurrence event, and new obligation.

The recurrence policy SHOULD distinguish a repeated symptom from a proven common root cause. Shared cause MUST NOT be inferred from superficial similarity alone.

## **12\. Canonical Authority and Commit Semantics**

### **12.1 Authority model**

EVIDENCE:  
  SUPPORTS CLAIMS

LLM:  
  OBSERVATION / PROPOSAL ONLY

VALIDATOR:  
  DETERMINISTIC STRUCTURAL AND CONTRACT CHECKS

REVIEW AUTHORITY:  
  MAKES GOVERNED DECISIONS WHERE REQUIRED

KERNEL:  
  SOLE CANONICAL COMMIT AUTHORITY

HISTORY:  
  CANONICAL RECORD OF COMMITTED EVENTS

REGISTRY / GRAPH / LEDGER PROJECTIONS:  
  DERIVED FROM CANONICAL HISTORY

The validator MUST NOT claim to prove semantic completeness beyond its demonstrated capabilities. An LLM-generated relationship or closure proposal MUST NOT become canonical solely because it is syntactically valid.

### **12.2 Commit contract**

Any lifecycle integrity certificate, obligation closure, ownership transfer, reopening, or canonical registry transition MUST follow:

PROPOSE  
  \-\> VALIDATE  
  \-\> AUTHORIZE, IF REQUIRED  
  \-\> COMMIT REQUEST  
  \-\> AUTHORITATIVE COMMIT RESULT  
  \-\> EFFECT / PROJECTION VERIFICATION

The exact number of stages may differ by event type, but their authority boundaries MUST remain explicit.

### **12.3 Commit ambiguity**

If the commit acknowledgment is missing, the system MUST reconcile authoritative History using the idempotency identity and canonical event position.

It MUST NOT:

* assume the commit failed;  
* issue an unguarded duplicate commit;  
* declare the obligation closed from a local projection;  
* delete the pending record;  
* replace an unknown result with success or failure without evidence.

### **12.4 Projection recovery**

A registry, dependency graph, or obligation ledger projection that diverges from History MUST be rebuilt or reconciled through an authorized projection-recovery process.

Projection recovery MUST NOT rewrite canonical events to match a corrupted projection.

## **13\. Determinism, Replay, and Reproducibility**

### **13.1 Deterministic audit inputs**

An audit result MUST be reproducible from the same:

* canonical History position;  
* validated input snapshot;  
* schema and contract revisions;  
* audit rule-set revision;  
* dependency-resolution rules;  
* policy revisions;  
* evidence manifest;  
* validator version;  
* declared scope and cutoff.

Where external evidence cannot be reproduced byte-for-byte, the evidence reference and integrity-verification method MUST be preserved.

### **13.2 Replay semantics**

Replay reconstructs derived state from canonical History. It MUST NOT execute corrective actions, send revocation messages, transfer ownership, close obligations, or issue closure certificates as external side effects.

A new audit run after replay is a separate operation with its own identity and evidence. Replay itself MUST NOT create a new certification decision.

### **13.3 Deterministic ordering**

Where multiple findings or obligations are presented in a deterministic report, the ordering rule MUST be explicit, such as a canonical identity ordering. Presentation ordering MUST NOT change adjudication priority, authorization, or closure semantics.

### **13.4 Audit disagreement**

If two valid audit runs using nominally equivalent inputs produce materially different decisions, the system MUST preserve both results and create a discrepancy finding.

The discrepancy MUST be investigated for input drift, rule-set differences, nondeterminism, implementation defects, or incomplete evidence. The system MUST NOT silently select the more permissive outcome.

## **14\. Security and Trust Boundaries**

### **14.1 Input trust**

Certificate records, consumer reports, dependency edges, evidence manifests, and closure claims MUST be treated as untrusted inputs until their applicable authenticity, integrity, authority, and scope checks pass.

A trusted transport does not automatically establish the semantic validity of its payload.

### **14.2 Identity and authority**

The system MUST validate that:

* each referenced identity resolves unambiguously;  
* the event issuer had the required authority;  
* the authority was valid for the relevant scope and time;  
* evidence signatures and trust chains satisfy the applicable policy;  
* revoked credentials are handled according to the governing trust rules;  
* authorization references are bound to the exact decision being committed.

### **14.3 Tamper evidence**

Changes to a certificate, lifecycle event, obligation record, or closure artifact MUST be detectable under the adopted integrity mechanism.

The precise cryptographic scheme, canonical serialization, and key-management requirements remain governed by the applicable security contracts. STEP-150 does not independently select or certify those mechanisms.

### **14.4 Privilege separation**

The authority that proposes or executes a corrective action MUST NOT automatically be treated as the authority that independently verifies its effect or accepts residual risk, unless an adopted policy explicitly permits that arrangement.

Any permitted role combination MUST be recorded and auditable.

## **15\. Normative Invariants**

INV-150-001: Every closure assessment is bound to an immutable input snapshot.  
INV-150-002: Canonical History is authoritative over registry and ledger projections.  
INV-150-003: Certificate identity and revision resolution follow explicit rules.  
INV-150-004: Historical certificate retention is distinct from operative-use eligibility.  
INV-150-005: Cross-certificate relationships require validated provenance.  
INV-150-006: Dependency completeness cannot be inferred from an empty result.  
INV-150-007: Conflicting operative certificates cannot be silently resolved.  
INV-150-008: Every mandatory residual obligation has an explicit lifecycle state.  
INV-150-009: Every mandatory obligation has an accountable owner or an explicit assignment obligation.  
INV-150-010: Obligation transfer does not erase age, evidence, or escalation history.  
INV-150-011: Action execution does not prove effect verification.  
INV-150-012: Effect verification does not independently authorize obligation closure.  
INV-150-013: Exceptions require explicit authority, scope, conditions, and evidence.  
INV-150-014: A replacement certificate does not discharge inherited obligations automatically.  
INV-150-015: Orphaned references are preserved as findings rather than silently deleted.  
INV-150-016: Closure requires a complete in-scope obligation inventory.  
INV-150-017: An incomplete or conflicted audit cannot produce unqualified closure.  
INV-150-018: Post-closure regression can trigger an auditable reopening.  
INV-150-019: Replay cannot execute external actions or mutate canonical History.  
INV-150-020: Lifecycle integrity certification does not authorize operational re-entry or Freeze.

An implementation claiming conformance MUST provide verification evidence for every applicable invariant. The existence of a validator, schema, or test file alone is insufficient evidence that the invariant holds.

## **16\. Test Candidates**

All tests below are proposed and have not been executed.

| Test ID | Scenario | Expected result |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| CC-150-T01 | Registry disagrees with canonical History | Detect divergence; History remains authoritative. |
| CC-150-T02 | Certificate identity resolves to conflicting records | Mark conflict; block unqualified closure. |
| CC-150-T03 | Superseded certificate remains historically referenced | Preserve history and evaluate whether the reference is still valid for its use. |
| CC-150-T04 | Two certificates appear operative for overlapping scope | Apply explicit compatibility rules; otherwise mark conflict. |
| CC-150-T05 | Dependency scan returns zero edges but is incomplete | Reject the completeness claim. |
| CC-150-T06 | Revocation is committed but a required consumer is unresolved | Keep propagation obligation open. |
| CC-150-T07 | Replacement certificate exists while an old obligation remains open | Prevent global closure. |
| CC-150-T08 | Obligation has no owner | Create or expose assignment obligation; do not imply ownership. |
| CC-150-T09 | Ownership transfer omits required acceptance evidence | Reject or keep transfer pending under policy. |
| CC-150-T10 | Corrective action executed without effect verification | Keep obligation open. |
| CC-150-T11 | Exception lacks scope or expiry | Reject exception authorization. |
| CC-150-T12 | Closed obligation's evidence is later invalidated | Reopen or create linked follow-up obligation. |
| CC-150-T13 | Orphaned reference points to an authorizedly retired target | Classify using lifecycle policy; do not delete the reference silently. |
| CC-150-T14 | Cross-certificate edge has a digest mismatch | Reject the edge as valid evidence. |
| CC-150-T15 | Audit snapshot changes during evaluation | Abort, restart, or revalidate against a consistent snapshot. |
| CC-150-T16 | Audit finds no open obligations but inventory completeness is unknown | Do not issue closure certification. |
| CC-150-T17 | Commit acknowledgment is missing | Reconcile History before retry or closure. |
| CC-150-T18 | Replay encounters an obligation closure event | Reconstruct state without executing closure side effects. |
| CC-150-T19 | A closed inconsistency recurs | Preserve prior closure and create a recurrence finding. |
| CC-150-T20 | Closure audit passes but operational re-entry gate is absent | Do not infer operational authorization. |

### **16.1 Additional negative-test requirements**

The test suite SHOULD additionally cover:

* cyclic dependency graphs where cycles are prohibited by the adopted graph policy;  
* cycles that are explicitly permitted but require special interpretation;  
* conflicting revocation and supersession events;  
* stale policy references in closure evidence;  
* one obligation incorrectly used to discharge two distinct requirements;  
* two duplicate obligations whose scopes differ;  
* evidence valid for one certificate revision but not another;  
* a replacement certificate with a narrower scope than its predecessor;  
* a residual obligation that reappears after authorized closure;  
* non-deterministic audit outcomes from equivalent frozen inputs.

The test oracle MUST identify the required state, evidence, and prohibited side effects for each case.

## **18\. Acceptance and Verification Gates**

| Gate | Required condition | Failure consequence |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| G150-01 | Cross-certificate snapshot is integrity-validated. | Block audit. |
| G150-02 | Certificate identities and revisions resolve deterministically. | Block closure. |
| G150-03 | Historical and operative states are distinguished. | Block operative-state certification. |
| G150-04 | Dependency graph scope and completeness are evidenced. | Block global consistency claims. |
| G150-05 | Cross-certificate relationships satisfy their type-specific contracts. | Record findings; block closure as applicable. |
| G150-06 | Revocation and supersession effects are reconciled. | Keep affected obligations open. |
| G150-07 | Residual obligation inventory is complete for the declared scope. | Block closure certification. |
| G150-08 | Mandatory obligations have valid ownership or assignment obligations. | Block unqualified closure. |
| G150-09 | Closure predicates are defined for each mandatory obligation type. | Block obligation closure. |
| G150-10 | Effect verification evidence is valid and scope-bound. | Keep obligation open. |
| G150-11 | Exceptions are authorized and satisfy their conditions. | Reject exception-based closure. |
| G150-12 | Orphaned and contradictory references are adjudicated or explicitly unresolved. | Block unqualified closure. |
| G150-13 | Audit inputs and rules are reproducible. | Block audit conformance. |
| G150-14 | Canonical commit and projection verification are separate. | Block committed-closure claims. |
| G150-15 | Reopening and recurrence handling are defined. | Block lifecycle-integrity adoption. |
| G150-16 | Replay has no external side effects. | Fail recovery conformance. |
| G150-17 | All applicable invariants have verification evidence. | Prohibit conformance claims. |
| G150-18 | Required tests pass with recorded evidence. | Keep implementation unverified. |
| G150-19 | Cross-contract conflicts and open issues are resolved or explicitly block dependent claims. | Block adoption of affected rules. |
| G150-20 | Operational re-entry and Freeze remain separate authorities. | Prohibit implicit authorization. |

## **21\. Final Adjudication**

### **21.1 Required closure chain**

FROZEN CROSS-CERTIFICATE SNAPSHOT  
  \-\> CERTIFICATE IDENTITY RESOLUTION  
  \-\> OPERATIVE-STATE RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> DEPENDENCY GRAPH VALIDATION  
  \-\> REVOCATION / SUPERSESSION RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> RESIDUAL OBLIGATION INVENTORY  
  \-\> OWNERSHIP AND DISPOSITION VALIDATION  
  \-\> EFFECT VERIFICATION  
  \-\> DOWNSTREAM AUTHORIZATION / OPERATION RECONCILIATION  
  \-\> LIFECYCLE INTEGRITY AUDIT  
  \-\> CLOSURE ELIGIBILITY DECISION  
  \-\> AUTHORIZED CANONICAL COMMIT  
  \-\> POST-COMMIT VERIFICATION  
  \-\> CONDITIONAL CLOSURE CERTIFICATION  
  \-\> POST-CLOSURE MONITORING AND REOPENING

### **21.2 Mandatory conclusions**

1. A certificate replacement does not discharge inherited obligations by itself.  
2. A complete inventory of discovered obligations does not prove that discovery was complete.  
3. A zero-finding audit does not prove lifecycle integrity unless its input scope and discovery process are validated.  
4. A committed closure decision does not prove that its effects and projections have been verified.  
5. A closure certificate is bounded by its snapshot, scope, policy, and evidence.  
6. Newly discovered dependencies or invalidated evidence may require reopening.  
7. All canonical changes remain subject to the Kernel's authorized commit process.  
8. Neither lifecycle integrity nor closure certification implicitly grants operational re-entry or Freeze.

## **0\. 規範上の位置づけ**

STEP-150 が定義する Cross-Certificate Closure Integrity、Residual Obligation Ledger、Dependency Reconciliation、および Closure Certificate を前提とする。

STEP-151 は、閉包証明の発行後に発生する変化を検出・分類し、証明の現行性を再評価し、必要に応じて無効化・失効・下流消費者への伝播・再閉包評価を行うための規範契約である。

本 STEP の中心的な対象は、閉包証明の発行時点に成立していた条件が、その後も成立しているかを監視し、成立しなくなった可能性がある場合に、証拠と権限に基づいて状態を再評価することである。

本 STEP は、監視の実行、リポジトリへの変更、証明の発行・失効、運用再開、Freeze を自動的に許可するものではない。

### **0.1 規範キーワード**

* `MUST`：必須。  
* `MUST NOT`：禁止。  
* `SHOULD`：例外の根拠を記録した場合を除き推奨。  
* `SHOULD NOT`：明示的な根拠がある場合を除き非推奨。  
* `MAY`：権限・ポリシー・契約上の許可範囲で選択可能。

### **0.2 状態の分離**

次の状態は、それぞれ独立した事実であり、相互に代替してはならない。

CLOSURE\_CERTIFICATE\_ISSUED  
    \!= CLOSURE\_CERTIFICATE\_PUBLISHED

CLOSURE\_CERTIFICATE\_PUBLISHED  
    \!= CONSUMER\_RECEIVED

CONSUMER\_RECEIVED  
    \!= CONSUMER\_ACKNOWLEDGED

CONSUMER\_ACKNOWLEDGED  
    \!= CONSUMER\_VALIDATED

CONSUMER\_VALIDATED  
    \!= CONSUMER\_ACCEPTED

CONSUMER\_ACCEPTED  
    \!= CERTIFICATE\_CURRENT

CLOSURE\_CURRENT\_AT\_ISSUANCE  
    \!= CURRENT\_NOW

NO\_DRIFT\_DETECTED  
    \!= NO\_DRIFT\_EXISTS

MONITORING\_WINDOW\_COMPLETE  
    \!= LIFETIME\_VALIDITY

DRIFT\_DETECTED  
    \!= CLOSURE\_INVALIDATED

INVALIDATION\_REQUESTED  
    \!= INVALIDATION\_COMMITTED

INVALIDATION\_COMMITTED  
    \!= INVALIDATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED

INVALIDATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
    \!= ALL\_CONSUMERS\_RECONCILED

NEW\_DEPENDENCY\_DISCOVERED  
    \!= DEPENDENCY\_CANONICALLY\_ACCEPTED

OBLIGATION\_RECURRENCE\_SUSPECTED  
    \!= RECURRENCE\_CONFIRMED

OBLIGATION\_REOPENED  
    \!= REMEDIATION\_EXECUTED

REMEDIATION\_EXECUTED  
    \!= REMEDIATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED

CLOSURE\_REVOKED  
    \!= ALL\_DOWNSTREAM\_USE\_PREVENTED

DOWNSTREAM\_AUTHORIZATION\_REVOKED  
    \!= IN\_FLIGHT\_OPERATION\_STOPPED

RE\_CLOSURE\_ELIGIBLE  
    \!= RE\_CLOSURE\_AUTHORIZED

RE\_CLOSURE\_COMMITTED  
    \!= RE\_CLOSURE\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED

REPLAY  
    \!= REEXECUTION

REPLAY  
    \!= CANONICAL\_MUTATION

COMMIT\_REQUESTED  
    \!= COMMIT\_COMPLETED

COMMIT\_ACK\_MISSING  
    \!= COMMIT\_FAILED

CLOSURE\_CERTIFIED  
    \!= OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED

OPERATIONAL\_REENTRY\_AUTHORIZED  
    \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZED

この区別を、すべての状態機械、イベント、監査記録、下流通知、判定結果に適用する。

## **1\. 目的**

STEP-151 は、次の問題を扱う。

1. 閉包証明発行後の依存グラフの変化。  
2. 新たな依存関係の発見と、未承認依存の残存。  
3. 解消済みとされた義務・問題の再発。  
4. 既検証効果の回帰。  
5. 証明が依拠する証拠・ポリシー・スコープの変化。  
6. 閉包証明の有効条件の喪失。  
7. 証明の無効化・失効の要求、確定、効果検証。  
8. 下流消費者への失効情報の伝播と個別状態の照合。  
9. 下流の許可、実行中操作、運用状態との不整合。  
10. 再閉包に向けた新しい基準スナップショットの構築。  
11. 監視不能・入力不完全・証拠不足の場合の保守的な状態管理。  
12. 閉包証明の再評価と、運用再開・Freeze の権限分離。

## **2\. 非目的**

STEP-151 は、次を実施しない。

* ソース仕様の再抽出。  
* 意味内容の自動修復。  
* LLM による意味的正当性の最終確定。  
* 依存関係候補の自動的な正規化・承認。  
* 義務の自動的な削除。  
* 証明の無条件な延命。  
* 失効通知の送信成功を前提とする分散原子的更新。  
* 通知未達を理由とした失効状態の取消し。  
* 運用再開の自動許可。  
* Freeze の自動許可。  
* 監視結果だけを根拠とした Canonical History の改変。  
* 証拠の不在から、問題の不存在を推論すること。  
* テスト未実行状態での実装適合性の主張。

## **3\. 権威モデル**

### **3.1 Canonical Truth**

Canonical Truth \= Authorized, Validated, Committed History  
Current State   \= fold(Canonical History)  
Projection      \= Derived(Canonical History)  
Observation     \= Non-canonical  
Proposal        \= Non-canonical  
Monitoring View \= Derived  
Audit Report    \= Derived, provenance-bound

* `History` は正規の変更事実を保持する。  
* `State` は History の fold によって導出する。  
* 監視結果、ダッシュボード、集計値は派生物である。  
* 監視結果から Canonical State への直接書き戻しは禁止する。  
* 正規の状態変更は、認可された Kernel の検証・Commit 経路のみで行う。

### **3.2 LLM の権限**

LLM は、次の候補を提示できる。

* 依存関係の変化候補。  
* 義務再発の候補。  
* 証拠の矛盾候補。  
* 閉包述語の回帰候補。  
* 影響範囲候補。  
* 再閉包に必要な追加調査候補。

LLM は、次を確定してはならない。

* Canonical dependency の追加・削除。  
* 義務の正式な再オープン。  
* 閉包証明の正式な無効化・失効。  
* 下流消費者の受領・受諾状態。  
* 運用再開許可。  
* Freeze 許可。

LLM が返す分類や確信度は、監査候補の優先順位付けに使用できるが、単独で正規状態の変更を正当化しない。

### **3.3 Validator の権限と限界**

決定論的 Validator は、実装された検証能力の範囲内で次を確認する。

* スキーマ適合性。  
* 必須フィールド。  
* 識別子の一意性。  
* ダイジェストの形式と比較。  
* 参照整合性。  
* 証拠の存在・形式・署名等の機械検証可能な条件。  
* 状態遷移の許可条件。  
* 認可情報の検証。  
* イベントの冪等性。  
* バージョンとスナップショットの整合性。  
* 必須監視対象の網羅性。

Validator は、意味論的な完全性や現実世界の真実性を、実装上の検証能力を超えて保証してはならない。

## **4\. 閉包証明の拘束条件**

### **4.1 証明の基準スナップショット**

各 Closure Certificate は、少なくとも次の情報に拘束されなければならない。

closure\_certificate:  
  certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  certificate\_revision: REQUIRED  
  predecessor\_certificate\_ids: \[\]  
  issuance\_event\_id: REQUIRED

  closure\_scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_graph\_digest: REQUIRED  
  obligation\_ledger\_digest: REQUIRED  
  source\_snapshot\_digest: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED  
  policy\_snapshot\_digest: REQUIRED  
  operational\_state\_digest: REQUIRED  
  consumer\_cohort\_digest: REQUIRED

  closure\_predicate\_results\_digest: REQUIRED  
  validation\_record\_digest: REQUIRED  
  authority\_context\_digest: REQUIRED

  issued\_at: REQUIRED  
  validity\_conditions: REQUIRED  
  monitoring\_policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  monitoring\_policy\_version: REQUIRED

  invalidation\_state: REQUIRED  
  revocation\_state: REQUIRED

上記は契約上の論理フィールドであり、実装済みスキーマの存在を意味しない。

### **4.2 証明の意味**

証明は、指定されたスコープ、基準スナップショット、ポリシー、証拠、閉包述語の組合せに対してのみ有効性を主張する。

Certificate validity  
  \= Closure predicates satisfied  
  AND Bound inputs verified  
  AND Required evidence accepted  
  AND Scope unchanged or formally re-evaluated  
  AND Policy conditions satisfied  
  AND No authoritative invalidation/revocation event  
  AND Currentness assessment sufficient for the requested decision

これは実装可能な判定モデルの提案であり、現実世界における絶対的な正当性の保証ではない。

### **4.3 現行性の期限・条件**

証明は、発行時点の状態を記録するものであり、発行後の永久的な現行性を保証しない。

現行性は、次の条件の組合せによって評価する。

* 有効期限または再評価期限。  
* 監視対象の変化イベント。  
* 必須データソースの可用性。  
* 依存グラフの改訂。  
* 義務台帳の改訂。  
* 証拠の失効・更新・信頼性変更。  
* ポリシー改訂。  
* 運用状態の変更。  
* 下流消費者の状態。  
* 閉包述語の再評価結果。

監視期間内に変化が検出されなかったことを、監視対象外の変化が存在しない証明として扱ってはならない。

## **5\. 監視対象と Drift 分類**

### **5.1 Drift 分類体系**

| 分類 | 意味 | 初期的な扱い |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| `GRAPH_TOPOLOGY_CHANGED` | ノード・エッジ構造の変化 | 影響範囲を再評価 |
| `NEW_EDGE_CANDIDATE` | 新規依存関係候補 | 未検証依存として保持 |
| `MISSING_EDGE_CANDIDATE` | 既存依存関係の欠落候補 | 削除の正当性を確認 |
| `CERTIFICATE_LIFECYCLE_CHANGED` | 関連証明の状態変化 | 依存する証明を照合 |
| `POLICY_CHANGED` | 判定ポリシーの改訂 | 新旧条件を比較 |
| `BASELINE_CHANGED` | 基準スナップショットの変化 | 基準の再構築を評価 |
| `SCOPE_CHANGED` | 対象範囲の変更 | 旧証明の適用範囲を再判定 |
| `CONSUMER_REGRESSION` | 下流消費者の状態回帰 | 個別照合を要求 |
| `EVIDENCE_REVOKED` | 証拠の失効・取消し | 依拠する述語を再評価 |
| `EVIDENCE_STALE` | 証拠の鮮度条件違反 | 有効性の再評価を要求 |
| `EVIDENCE_TRUST_CHANGED` | 信頼性・署名・発行者状態の変化 | 信頼判定を再実行 |
| `VERIFIED_EFFECT_REGRESSION` | 検証済み効果の回帰 | 効果の再検証を要求 |
| `OBLIGATION_RECURRENCE_CANDIDATE` | 解消済み義務の再発候補 | 再発判定へ |
| `ORPHANED_REFERENCE` | 参照先の欠落 | 参照整合性監査へ |
| `AUTHORIZATION_OPERATION_DIVERGENCE` | 許可状態と実行状態の不一致 | 安全な対処を別途判断 |
| `CLOSURE_PREDICATE_REGRESSION` | 閉包述語の再評価失敗 | 再閉包評価へ |
| `SOURCE_SNAPSHOT_INVALIDATED` | 依拠するソーススナップショットの無効化 | 影響証明を特定 |
| `MONITORING_COVERAGE_GAP` | 監視対象の未観測・未照合 | 完全性を主張しない |
| `UNKNOWN_DRIFT` | 分類不能な変化 | 不明状態を維持 |

複数分類の同時付与を許可する。ただし、各分類の根拠を別々に記録する。

### **5.2 監視の入力**

各監視実行は、次を識別する。

monitoring\_input:  
  monitoring\_run\_id: REQUIRED  
  target\_certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  target\_certificate\_revision: REQUIRED

  expected\_scope\_digest: REQUIRED  
  expected\_graph\_digest: REQUIRED  
  expected\_obligation\_ledger\_digest: REQUIRED  
  expected\_policy\_digest: REQUIRED  
  expected\_evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED  
  expected\_operational\_state\_digest: REQUIRED  
  expected\_consumer\_cohort\_digest: REQUIRED

  observed\_source\_manifests: REQUIRED  
  observed\_event\_range: REQUIRED  
  observation\_cutoff: REQUIRED

  required\_sources: REQUIRED  
  observed\_sources: REQUIRED  
  unavailable\_sources: REQUIRED  
  freshness\_results: REQUIRED  
  coverage\_results: REQUIRED

必須ソースが欠落している場合、その監視実行は `VALID` として完了してはならない。

### **5.3 Drift の確度**

監視結果は、少なくとも次の段階を区別する。

OBSERVED\_CHANGE  
    ↓  
DRIFT\_CANDIDATE  
    ↓  
DRIFT\_CONFIRMED  
    ↓  
IMPACT\_ASSESSED  
    ↓  
CERTIFICATE\_REASSESSMENT\_REQUIRED  
    ↓  
INVALIDATION\_DECISION

各段階は独立したイベントである。後段の判定が前段の記録を上書きしてはならない。

`DRIFT_CONFIRMED` は、必ずしも閉包証明の無効化を意味しない。変化が証明のスコープ外である場合や、閉包述語に影響しないことが適切な証拠で確認された場合は、その根拠を記録して判定を終了できる。

ただし、影響が不明である場合、影響なしとして処理してはならない。

## **6\. 監視状態機械**

### **6.1 状態一覧**

NOT\_SCHEDULED  
SCHEDULED  
RUNNING  
INPUT\_VALIDATION\_PENDING  
VALID  
INCOMPLETE  
STALE  
CONFLICTED  
UNKNOWN  
DRIFT\_CANDIDATE  
DRIFT\_CONFIRMED  
REASSESSMENT\_REQUIRED  
INVALIDATION\_PENDING  
INVALIDATED  
REVOCATION\_PENDING  
REVOKED  
RECONCILIATION\_PENDING  
RECONCILED  
FAILED  
EXHAUSTED

### **6.2 状態の定義**

| 状態 | 定義 |
| ----- | ----- |
| `NOT_SCHEDULED` | 監視計画が未作成 |
| `SCHEDULED` | 監視計画が登録済み |
| `RUNNING` | 監視処理を実行中 |
| `INPUT_VALIDATION_PENDING` | 入力検証が未完了 |
| `VALID` | 必須入力・対象・観測範囲の検証が成功 |
| `INCOMPLETE` | 必須データまたは対象の一部が未観測 |
| `STALE` | 必要な鮮度条件を満たさない |
| `CONFLICTED` | 同一対象について矛盾する観測が存在 |
| `UNKNOWN` | 現行性を判定できない |
| `DRIFT_CANDIDATE` | 変化の候補を検出 |
| `DRIFT_CONFIRMED` | 定義済みの判定条件で変化を確認 |
| `REASSESSMENT_REQUIRED` | 閉包述語または影響範囲の再評価が必要 |
| `INVALIDATION_PENDING` | 無効化判断または Commit が未完了 |
| `INVALIDATED` | 無効化が Canonical History に確定済み |
| `REVOCATION_PENDING` | 失効処理が未完了 |
| `REVOKED` | 失効が Canonical History に確定済み |
| `RECONCILIATION_PENDING` | 下流消費者との照合が未完了 |
| `RECONCILED` | 定義された必須消費者の照合が完了 |
| `FAILED` | 回復可能性を含め、処理失敗が記録済み |
| `EXHAUSTED` | ポリシーで定めた再試行・監視予算を使い切った |

### **6.3 状態遷移の制約**

1. `VALID` は、入力・観測範囲・鮮度の条件を満たした場合に限る。  
2. `INCOMPLETE`、`STALE`、`CONFLICTED`、`UNKNOWN` は、`VALID` と同義に扱わない。  
3. `DRIFT_CANDIDATE` から `DRIFT_CONFIRMED` への遷移は、規定の確認根拠を必要とする。  
4. `DRIFT_CONFIRMED` から `INVALIDATED` への遷移は、認可された無効化 Commit を必要とする。  
5. `INVALIDATION_PENDING` は、無効化の完了を意味しない。  
6. `INVALIDATED` は、下流消費者への伝播完了を意味しない。  
7. `REVOKED` は、すべての下流環境での利用停止を意味しない。  
8. `RECONCILED` は、定義済みの消費者集合に対する照合完了を意味する。未登録消費者の不存在は保証しない。  
9. `EXHAUSTED` は、対象が正常であることを意味しない。  
10. 不明状態から正常状態への遷移は、再検証に必要な証拠が揃った場合に限る。

## **7\. 新規依存関係の発見と確定**

### **7.1 発見から Canonical Commit まで**

新規依存関係は、次の段階を経る。

DISCOVERED  
    ↓  
DISCOVERED\_UNVALIDATED  
    ↓  
IDENTITY\_AND\_PROVENANCE\_VALIDATED  
    ↓  
SCOPE\_AND\_AUTHORITY\_CHECKED  
    ↓  
IMPACT\_ASSESSED  
    ↓  
CANONICAL\_COMMIT\_REQUESTED  
    ↓  
CANONICAL\_COMMIT\_CONFIRMED  
    ↓  
DEPENDENCY\_GRAPH\_REBUILT  
    ↓  
AFFECTED\_CERTIFICATES\_REASSESSED

各段階で必要な証拠と権限が欠落している場合、後段へ進めてはならない。

### **7.2 未承認依存の扱い**

`DISCOVERED_UNVALIDATED` の依存候補は、監査上の候補として保持する。

ただし、候補の存在を理由に、その依存関係が Canonical Graph に確定したと主張してはならない。

同時に、未承認依存の候補が存在する場合、監視対象範囲が十分に網羅されていると証明できない限り、完全性の主張を制限する。

dependency\_candidate:  
  candidate\_id: REQUIRED  
  source\_node\_id: REQUIRED  
  target\_node\_id: REQUIRED  
  relation\_type: REQUIRED

  discovery\_event\_id: REQUIRED  
  discovery\_source\_digest: REQUIRED  
  provenance: REQUIRED

  validation\_status: REQUIRED  
  authority\_check\_status: REQUIRED  
  impact\_assessment\_status: REQUIRED

  canonical\_commit\_event\_id: null  
  disposition: REQUIRED

`canonical_commit_event_id` が存在しない候補を、Canonical Graph の確定エッジとして扱ってはならない。

### **7.3 依存グラフ再構築**

新規依存が確定した場合、再構築後のグラフは新しいバージョンまたはダイジェストで識別する。

旧グラフのダイジェストを維持したまま、新しいエッジが含まれていると主張してはならない。

依存グラフの変化によって影響を受ける証明は、明示的に列挙する。影響対象を完全に特定できない場合は、その不確実性を記録する。

## **8\. Residual Obligation Recurrence Detection**

### **8.1 再発の定義**

再発とは、過去に解消済みと判定された義務または問題について、その解消条件が再び満たされなくなった、または新たな証拠によって解消判定の前提が成立しなくなった可能性を指す。

次の事象を区別する。

* 同一義務の再発。  
* 同一原因に由来する別義務の発生。  
* 類似した症状の再出現。  
* 過去の解消判定の誤りを示す新証拠。  
* 過去の義務と無関係な新規問題。  
* 判定不能な類似事象。

類似した症状だけを根拠に、同一義務の再発を確定してはならない。

### **8.2 再発レコード**

obligation\_recurrence:  
  recurrence\_id: REQUIRED  
  prior\_obligation\_id: REQUIRED  
  prior\_closure\_event\_id: REQUIRED

  candidate\_event\_id: REQUIRED  
  candidate\_evidence\_manifest\_digest: REQUIRED

  identity\_assessment: REQUIRED  
  recurrence\_classification: REQUIRED  
  causal\_relation\_status: REQUIRED

  evidence\_for\_recurrence: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_against\_recurrence: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_questions: REQUIRED

  adjudication\_status: REQUIRED  
  adjudicator\_identity: REQUIRED  
  adjudication\_event\_id: null

  disposition: REQUIRED  
  resulting\_obligation\_id: null

`evidence_against_recurrence` と `unresolved_questions` は、ポリシー上適用可能な場合に省略してはならない。

### **8.3 義務の再オープン**

再発が確定した場合、次のいずれかを選択する。

1. 過去の義務を再オープンする。  
2. 新しい義務を作成し、過去の義務との関係を記録する。  
3. 同一義務ではないと判定し、独立した義務として登録する。  
4. 十分な証拠がないため判定保留とする。

選択は、義務の同一性規則、権限、履歴との整合性に従う。

過去の閉包イベントを削除・上書きしてはならない。再発は新しい履歴イベントとして追記する。

### **8.4 再発と閉包証明への影響**

義務の再発が確認された場合、少なくとも次を再評価する。

* 当該義務に依存する閉包述語。  
* 関連する証明のスコープ。  
* 以前の解消証拠の有効性。  
* 同一の証拠に依存する他の義務。  
* 下流証明との依存関係。  
* 運用状態への影響。  
* 既検証効果が依然として成立しているか。

再発が証明の閉包条件に影響する場合、再評価を開始する。無効化・失効の確定は、別途認可された判断と Commit に従う。

## **9\. Closure Certificate の無効化と失効**

### **9.1 用語**

`INVALIDATION` は、証明の基準・根拠・閉包条件が成立しなくなった、または成立を確認できないことにより、証明の有効性主張を維持できないと判定する処理である。

`REVOCATION` は、認可された権限が証明の利用を今後認めないことを正規履歴に確定する処理である。

両者は異なる。無効化と失効を一つのフラグで代用してはならない。

### **9.2 判断条件**

無効化・失効の判断では、少なくとも次を考慮する。

* 閉包述語の失敗。  
* 証明の基準スナップショットの無効化。  
* 必須証拠の失効。  
* 証拠の改ざんまたは信頼性喪失。  
* 依存グラフの変更。  
* 未解決義務の発見または再発。  
* ポリシー変更による適用条件の不成立。  
* スコープの変更。  
* 権限の取消し。  
* 誤発行・重複発行。  
* 必須データの欠落による現行性の判定不能。  
* 事前定義された失効条件への該当。

現行性が不明であることと、証明が確実に無効であることを区別する。ポリシーが判定不能時の失効を要求する場合は、そのポリシーと根拠を明示する。

### **9.3 無効化・失効ライフサイクル**

DRIFT\_CONFIRMED  
    ↓  
IMPACT\_ASSESSED  
    ↓  
INVALIDATION\_DECISION\_PENDING  
    ↓  
INVALIDATION\_AUTHORIZED  
    ↓  
INVALIDATION\_COMMIT\_REQUESTED  
    ↓  
INVALIDATION\_COMMITTED  
    ↓  
INVALIDATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFICATION\_PENDING  
    ↓  
INVALIDATION\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED  
    ↓  
REVOCATION\_DECISION\_PENDING  
    ↓  
REVOCATION\_AUTHORIZED  
    ↓  
REVOCATION\_COMMIT\_REQUESTED  
    ↓  
REVOCATION\_COMMITTED  
    ↓  
CONSUMER\_RECONCILIATION\_PENDING  
    ↓  
CONSUMER\_RECONCILIATION\_COMPLETED

このライフサイクルは、すべてのケースで全段階を必ず実行するという意味ではない。ポリシー上不要な段階は、その省略条件と根拠を記録する。

### **9.4 Commit の不確定性**

Commit 要求後に応答がない場合、Commit が失敗したと断定してはならない。

COMMIT\_REQUESTED  
    ↓  
ACK\_RECEIVED  
    → COMMIT\_CONFIRMED

COMMIT\_REQUESTED  
    ↓  
ACK\_MISSING  
    → COMMIT\_OUTCOME\_UNKNOWN  
    → HISTORY\_RECONCILIATION\_REQUIRED

同一の操作を再送する場合は、冪等性キーまたは同等の仕組みを使用し、二重の正規イベントを作成しない。

### **9.5 失効イベントの永続性**

`REVOCATION_COMMITTED` が確認された後に、下流通知が失敗しても、正規の失効状態を自動的に取り消してはならない。

下流伝播の失敗は、失効状態を保持したまま別の未完了タスクとして記録する。

REVOCATION\_COMMITTED  
    \!= REVOCATION\_PROPAGATION\_COMPLETED

## **10\. Downstream Consumer Reconciliation**

### **10.1 消費者集合**

監視対象の下流消費者は、基準スナップショット時点で定義された集合に基づいて管理する。

consumer\_cohort:  
  cohort\_id: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_revision: REQUIRED  
  cohort\_digest: REQUIRED

  required\_consumers: REQUIRED  
  optional\_consumers: REQUIRED  
  excluded\_consumers: REQUIRED

  inclusion\_policy\_digest: REQUIRED  
  exclusion\_authority: REQUIRED

消費者を対象集合から除外する場合、除外理由、権限、適用開始時点を記録する。必須消費者を、応答がないことだけを理由に除外してはならない。

### **10.2 消費者ごとの状態**

NOT\_NOTIFIED  
NOTIFICATION\_PENDING  
NOTIFICATION\_SENT  
DELIVERY\_CONFIRMED  
DELIVERY\_UNKNOWN  
RECEIVED  
ACKNOWLEDGED  
VALIDATION\_PENDING  
VALIDATED  
ACCEPTANCE\_PENDING  
ACCEPTED  
RECONCILIATION\_REQUIRED  
RECONCILED  
CONFLICTED  
FAILED  
EXCLUDED\_BY\_AUTHORITY

状態は消費者ごとに記録する。ある消費者の成功を、他の消費者の成功として扱ってはならない。

### **10.3 通知と確認**

通知送信成功は、受領を意味しない。

受領確認は、通知内容が検証済みであることを意味しない。

検証成功は、消費者が失効情報を受諾し、実際の利用を停止したことを意味しない。

したがって、下流照合には、消費者の種類に応じて次の確認を要求する。

* 通知の送信状態。  
* 受領状態。  
* 対象証明 ID とリビジョン。  
* 失効イベント ID。  
* 消費者側の検証結果。  
* 消費者側の受諾・適用状態。  
* 旧証明に依存するキャッシュや派生物の処理状態。  
* 旧証明を根拠とする許可・操作の状態。  
* 確認に使用した証拠と時点。

### **10.4 照合完了条件**

必須消費者の状態が不明、未受領、未検証、または未照合の場合、全必須消費者の照合が完了したと主張してはならない。

`RECONCILED` の意味は、対象となる消費者集合と照合条件に限定する。

未知の消費者、登録外のコピー、外部システムへの流出、未登録キャッシュが存在しないことまでは保証しない。

### **10.5 分散処理**

分散システムにおける次の処理を、単一の原子的操作と仮定してはならない。

* 正規履歴への失効 Commit。  
* 通知の送信。  
* 通知の受領。  
* 消費者の検証。  
* 消費者側の利用停止。  
* 実行中操作の停止。  
* 停止効果の検証。

Outbox/Inbox、再試行キュー、冪等性キー、配信記録、照合ジョブ等の方式は採用候補であり、特定方式の採用は別途決定する。

## **11\. Authorization / In-Flight Operation の分離**

### **11.1 許可と実行の不一致**

証明が失効した場合でも、その証明を参照する許可、ジョブ、プロセス、キャッシュ、外部操作が即座に停止したとは限らない。

したがって、次を別々に追跡する。

CERTIFICATE\_STATUS  
AUTHORIZATION\_STATUS  
OPERATION\_STATUS  
EFFECT\_VERIFICATION\_STATUS  
CONSUMER\_RECONCILIATION\_STATUS

### **11.2 必要な対処**

失効の影響評価において、実行中操作への影響がある場合は、次を個別に判断する。

1. 新規操作の許可を停止する必要があるか。  
2. 既存許可を取り消す必要があるか。  
3. 実行中操作を継続・停止・隔離する必要があるか。  
4. 安全状態への移行が必要か。  
5. 停止または隔離を実行する権限があるか。  
6. 実行結果を検証する方法があるか。  
7. 操作の影響を受ける証拠・義務・証明は何か。

この判断は、関連する安全性・運用契約に従う。

### **11.3 安全状態**

安全状態への移行が必要な場合、検出結果だけで自動実行してはならない。実行には、別途認可されたポリシー、対象、操作、効果確認条件を必要とする。

緊急時の自動動作を許可する設計を採用する場合も、対象操作、許可条件、停止条件、権限、冪等性、監査要件を明示しなければならない。

## **12\. Re-Closure Baseline Reconstruction**

### **12.1 再閉包の前提**

再閉包は、旧証明を再利用して状態を正常扱いする処理ではない。

再閉包では、変化後の状態に基づき、新しい基準スナップショットを構築する。

REASSESSMENT\_REQUIRED  
    ↓  
IMPACT\_SCOPE\_IDENTIFIED  
    ↓  
NEW\_BASELINE\_CAPTURED  
    ↓  
BASELINE\_INPUTS\_VALIDATED  
    ↓  
DEPENDENCY\_GRAPH\_RECONCILED  
    ↓  
OBLIGATION\_LEDGER\_RECONCILED  
    ↓  
EVIDENCE\_MANIFEST\_REVALIDATED  
    ↓  
POLICY\_AND\_SCOPE\_REVALIDATED  
    ↓  
CLOSURE\_PREDICATES\_REEVALUATED  
    ↓  
RE\_CLOSURE\_ELIGIBILITY\_ASSESSED  
    ↓  
RE\_CLOSURE\_AUTHORIZATION\_PENDING  
    ↓  
RE\_CLOSURE\_COMMIT\_PENDING  
    ↓  
RE\_CLOSURE\_COMMITTED  
    ↓  
RE\_CLOSURE\_EFFECT\_VERIFICATION\_PENDING  
    ↓  
RE\_CLOSURE\_EFFECT\_VERIFIED

### **12.2 新基準の要件**

新しい基準スナップショットは、少なくとも次を含む。

* 新しいスナップショット ID。  
* 対象スコープ。  
* 依存グラフのダイジェスト。  
* 義務台帳のダイジェスト。  
* 証拠マニフェストのダイジェスト。  
* ポリシースナップショットのダイジェスト。  
* 運用状態のダイジェスト。  
* 閉包述語の評価結果。  
* 未解決事項。  
* 監視網羅性の結果。  
* 旧証明との関係。  
* 新しい証明の発行を許可する権限と条件。

### **12.3 再閉包の禁止条件**

次のいずれかに該当する場合、ポリシーが明示的な例外を定義しない限り、再閉包を確定してはならない。

* 必須入力が欠落している。  
* 依存グラフの不整合が解決されていない。  
* 必須義務が未解決である。  
* 必須証拠が失効または検証不能である。  
* 閉包述語が不成立である。  
* 必須消費者との矛盾が未解決である。  
* 権限の有効性を確認できない。  
* 新基準の完全性を評価できない。  
* Commit 結果が不明である。  
* 再閉包条件が定義されていない。

### **12.4 再閉包後の状態**

新しい閉包証明が発行されても、旧証明の履歴は保持する。

新証明と旧証明の関係を明示する。

OLD\_CERTIFICATE  
  ├── SUPERSEDED\_BY  
  ├── INVALIDATED\_BY  
  ├── REVOKED\_BY  
  └── REASSESSED\_BY

NEW\_CERTIFICATE  
  ├── BASELINE\_SNAPSHOT  
  ├── REASSESSMENT\_RECORD  
  └── CLOSURE\_EVIDENCE\_MANIFEST

すべての関係を同時に付与する必要はない。実際に成立する関係だけを記録する。

## **13\. Evidence Freshness / Trust Revalidation**

### **13.1 証拠の再評価**

証拠マニフェストは、証拠の存在だけでなく、その証拠が現在の判断に利用できるかを評価するための情報を持たなければならない。

evidence\_record:  
  evidence\_id: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_digest: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_type: REQUIRED  
  provenance: REQUIRED

  issuer\_identity: null  
  issuer\_trust\_status: REQUIRED

  observed\_at: REQUIRED  
  issued\_at: null  
  expires\_at: null

  freshness\_policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  trust\_policy\_id: REQUIRED

  revocation\_status: REQUIRED  
  integrity\_status: REQUIRED  
  applicability\_scope\_digest: REQUIRED

  verification\_record\_id: REQUIRED

日時フィールドは、情報源が提供する事実と、監査システムが観測した事実を混同してはならない。

### **13.2 鮮度判定**

鮮度は、単純な経過時間だけで決定するとは限らない。

適用されるポリシーに応じて、次を評価する。

* 最大許容経過時間。  
* イベント駆動の再検証条件。  
* 発行元の信頼状態。  
* 失効情報の取得状態。  
* 証拠の適用スコープ。  
* 証拠の生成条件。  
* 依拠するソースの改訂。  
* 証拠の整合性と署名。  
* 外部検証ソースの可用性。  
* 監査の時点と対象期間。

### **13.3 証拠ソースの停止**

必要な外部ソースを取得できない場合、未取得を「失効していない」「変更されていない」と同一視してはならない。

結果は `UNKNOWN`、`INCOMPLETE`、または `STALE` 等として記録し、適用ポリシーに従って再試行・エスカレーション・保守的な制限を行う。

## **14\. 監視ポリシーと網羅性**

### **14.1 必須ポリシー項目**

monitoring\_policy:  
  policy\_id: REQUIRED  
  version: REQUIRED  
  policy\_digest: REQUIRED

  monitored\_certificate\_classes: REQUIRED  
  required\_sources: REQUIRED  
  required\_consumers: REQUIRED

  scheduled\_interval: null  
  event\_triggers: REQUIRED  
  freshness\_constraints: REQUIRED  
  coverage\_constraints: REQUIRED

  retry\_policy: REQUIRED  
  timeout\_policy: REQUIRED  
  escalation\_policy: REQUIRED

  drift\_confirmation\_rules: REQUIRED  
  false\_positive\_adjudication: REQUIRED  
  false\_negative\_risk\_policy: REQUIRED

  invalidation\_conditions: REQUIRED  
  revocation\_conditions: REQUIRED  
  reclosure\_conditions: REQUIRED

### **14.2 定期監視とイベント駆動監視**

定期監視とイベント駆動監視は併用可能である。

ただし、どちらの方式も、監視間隔の間に起きたすべての変化を必ず検出するとは限らない。

イベントソースの欠落、順序逆転、遅延、重複、履歴の欠落を検知できない場合は、その限界を明示する。

### **14.3 監視網羅性**

網羅性の主張は、少なくとも次の対象集合に対して定義する。

* 必須依存ノード。  
* 必須依存エッジ。  
* 関連証明。  
* 必須義務。  
* 必須証拠ソース。  
* 必須ポリシー。  
* 必須下流消費者。  
* 必須運用状態。  
* 必須イベント範囲。

対象集合が未定義の場合、完全性の主張をしてはならない。

## **15\. 監査レコードとイベントモデル**

### **15.1 イベントの必須属性**

closure\_monitoring\_event:  
  event\_id: REQUIRED  
  event\_type: REQUIRED  
  event\_schema\_version: REQUIRED

  target\_certificate\_id: REQUIRED  
  target\_certificate\_revision: REQUIRED

  observed\_at: REQUIRED  
  committed\_at: null

  source\_snapshot\_digest: REQUIRED  
  monitoring\_policy\_digest: REQUIRED

  predecessor\_event\_ids: \[\]  
  correlation\_id: REQUIRED  
  idempotency\_key: REQUIRED

  actor\_identity: REQUIRED  
  authority\_context\_digest: REQUIRED

  evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  decision: REQUIRED  
  decision\_reason: REQUIRED

  validation\_record\_id: REQUIRED  
  outcome\_status: REQUIRED

### **15.2 イベントの追記**

イベントは、正規履歴に Commit されるまでは候補・観測・要求として扱う。

履歴に Commit されたイベントは、権限と履歴訂正ポリシーに従わない限り、直接変更・削除してはならない。

誤りの訂正は、元イベントを消去するのではなく、訂正・取消し・補足を示す新しいイベントとして表現する。

### **15.3 冪等性**

同じ監視結果または操作要求が複数回届いた場合、冪等性規則に従って重複効果を防止する。

冪等性キーの衝突は、同一操作の重複として安易に統合してはならない。対象、操作種別、スコープ、ペイロード、権限、基準リビジョンを比較する。

## **16\. Failure / Recovery / Replay**

### **16.1 失敗分類**

少なくとも次を区別する。

INPUT\_INVALID  
INPUT\_INCOMPLETE  
INPUT\_STALE  
SOURCE\_UNAVAILABLE  
SOURCE\_CONFLICT  
GRAPH\_RECONCILIATION\_FAILED  
OBLIGATION\_RECONCILIATION\_FAILED  
EVIDENCE\_VERIFICATION\_FAILED  
AUTHORIZATION\_FAILED  
COMMIT\_OUTCOME\_UNKNOWN  
COMMIT\_FAILED\_CONFIRMED  
NOTIFICATION\_FAILED  
CONSUMER\_ACK\_MISSING  
CONSUMER\_VALIDATION\_FAILED  
EFFECT\_VERIFICATION\_FAILED  
RE\_CLOSURE\_FAILED  
RETRY\_EXHAUSTED

### **16.2 回復**

回復処理は、失敗した段階とその正規状態を確認したうえで行う。

失効 Commit が成功している可能性がある場合、履歴照合前に同じ失効操作を無条件に再 Commit してはならない。

通知が失敗した場合は通知を再試行できるが、再試行のたびに新しい失効イベントを生成してはならない。

### **16.3 Replay**

Replay は、記録済みイベントから過去の判定状態を再構築するためのものである。

REPLAY:  
  READ\_CANONICAL\_HISTORY \= ALLOWED  
  RECONSTRUCT\_DERIVED\_STATE \= ALLOWED  
  REPRODUCE\_AUDIT\_VIEW \= ALLOWED  
  EXTERNAL\_NOTIFICATION \= FORBIDDEN\_BY\_DEFAULT  
  OPERATIONAL\_ACTION \= FORBIDDEN\_BY\_DEFAULT  
  CANONICAL\_MUTATION \= FORBIDDEN

外部効果を伴う操作は、通常の Replay から分離する。明示的な実行要求、認可、冪等性、対象検証がある場合にのみ、別の実行経路で処理する。

## **17\. セキュリティと監査可能性**

1. 証明の無効化・失効は、権限を検証した主体だけが要求・確定できる。  
2. 監視結果を改ざんしても、Canonical History を直接変更できてはならない。  
3. 署名・ダイジェストの検証は、定義済みアルゴリズムと正規化規則に従う。  
4. 証明 ID、リビジョン、スコープを省略した失効通知を、対象特定済みと扱ってはならない。  
5. 消費者が提示する受領確認は、対象イベントと対象証明に結び付ける。  
6. 消費者からの応答は、認証・整合性検証前に信頼してはならない。  
7. 時刻の差異や時刻ソースの信頼性を無視してはならない。  
8. 監査ログの機密情報は、アクセス制御・最小開示・保持ポリシーに従う。  
9. 失効状態を隠す、通知を偽装する、監視対象を無断で除外する操作を検出可能にする。  
10. 監視結果と正規状態の間の不一致を、監査対象として保持する。

## **18\. Normative Invariants（20項目）**

| ID | 不変条件 |
| ----- | ----- |
| `S151-I01` | Closure Certificate は発行時の基準スナップショットに拘束される。 |
| `S151-I02` | 発行時点の現行性を、現在の現行性として自動継承してはならない。 |
| `S151-I03` | 観測範囲の欠落から Drift 不存在を導出してはならない。 |
| `S151-I04` | `DRIFT_CANDIDATE` を `DRIFT_CONFIRMED` と同一視してはならない。 |
| `S151-I05` | Drift の検出だけで無効化・失効を確定してはならない。 |
| `S151-I06` | 無効化・失効は、認可された正規 Commit を必要とする。 |
| `S151-I07` | Commit 結果が不明な場合、失敗と断定してはならない。 |
| `S151-I08` | 失効の正規確定後、通知失敗を理由に失効を取り消してはならない。 |
| `S151-I09` | 下流消費者の受領状態は、消費者ごとに追跡する。 |
| `S151-I10` | 必須消費者の未照合状態を、全体照合完了として扱ってはならない。 |
| `S151-I11` | 消費者集合の変更は、認可と履歴を必要とする。 |
| `S151-I12` | 新規依存候補を、正規 Commit 前に Canonical Graph の確定エッジとして扱ってはならない。 |
| `S151-I13` | 依存関係の変更は、影響する閉包証明との関係を記録する。 |
| `S151-I14` | 義務の再発は、過去の閉包履歴を削除・上書きせず記録する。 |
| `S151-I15` | 症状の類似だけで義務の同一性・再発を確定してはならない。 |
| `S151-I16` | 証拠の取得失敗を、証拠の有効性確認成功として扱ってはならない。 |
| `S151-I17` | 証明失効を、下流操作の停止完了として扱ってはならない。 |
| `S151-I18` | 再閉包は、新しい基準と再検証済み証拠に基づく。 |
| `S151-I19` | Replay は、明示的に分離・認可されない限り外部効果および Canonical Mutation を発生させない。 |
| `S151-I20` | 閉包証明、運用再開許可、Freeze 許可は別々の権限・判定として保持する。 |

## **19\. Test Candidates（20項目）**

以下はテスト候補であり、実行済みテストではない。

| ID | テスト対象 | 期待結果 |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| `S151-T01` | 必須入力欠落 | `INCOMPLETE` または入力拒否。`VALID` にしない。 |
| `S151-T02` | 入力ダイジェスト不一致 | 入力検証失敗。対象証明を無条件に有効としない。 |
| `S151-T03` | 依存グラフの新規エッジ候補 | 未承認候補として保持する。 |
| `S151-T04` | 新規依存の認可済み Commit | 新しいグラフ識別子を生成し、影響証明を再評価する。 |
| `S151-T05` | Drift 候補のみ検出 | `DRIFT_CONFIRMED` に自動昇格しない。 |
| `S151-T06` | Drift 確認後に影響なしを検証 | 根拠を記録し、ポリシーに従って判定する。 |
| `S151-T07` | 閉包述語の回帰 | 再評価を要求する。 |
| `S151-T08` | 証拠の失効通知 | 依拠する証明・述語の影響範囲を特定する。 |
| `S151-T09` | 必須証拠ソース停止 | `UNKNOWN` 等を記録し、正常確認扱いにしない。 |
| `S151-T10` | 解消済み義務と類似症状 | 同一義務として自動確定しない。 |
| `S151-T11` | 義務の再発確定 | 旧履歴を維持し、新しいイベントを追加する。 |
| `S151-T12` | 無効化 Commit の応答欠落 | Commit 結果を不明とし、履歴照合を要求する。 |
| `S151-T13` | 失効 Commit 後の通知失敗 | 失効を保持し、伝播を別タスクとして再試行する。 |
| `S151-T14` | 必須消費者の ACK 欠落 | 全体照合を完了扱いにしない。 |
| `S151-T15` | 消費者から誤った証明 ID の ACK | 対象不一致として拒否または隔離する。 |
| `S151-T16` | 必須消費者の除外要求 | 権限と履歴がない場合は除外を拒否する。 |
| `S151-T17` | 失効済み証明を参照する実行中操作 | 操作停止を自動的に成功扱いせず、別途確認する。 |
| `S151-T18` | 再閉包時の未解決義務 | ポリシー上の例外がなければ再閉包を拒否する。 |
| `S151-T19` | Replay 中の外部通知要求 | 外部効果を発生させない。 |
| `S151-T20` | 閉包証明の発行後に Freeze 要求 | 閉包証明だけを根拠に Freeze を許可しない。 |

### **24.2 裁定根拠**

本契約は、閉包証明の発行後における現行性、依存グラフの変化、義務の再発、失効伝播、再閉包評価を規範的に分離した設計ドラフトである。

しかし、次の事実は確認されていない。

* 既存 STEP 群との完全な契約整合性。  
* 実際のリポジトリ構造との整合性。  
* 既存スキーマとの互換性。  
* 状態機械の実装可能性。  
* テストによる適合性。  
* 監視ソースの実在性・網羅性。  
* 失効処理と下流消費者の実運用能力。

したがって、`DRAFT_COMPLETE` はドラフト作成上の状態であり、`NORMATIVE_ACCEPTED`、`IMPLEMENTED`、`TESTED`、`FREEZE_READY` を意味しない。

### **24.3 次段階への進行条件**

STEP-152 へ進む場合も、STEP-151 の採用・実装・検証が完了したと仮定してはならない。STEP-152 は、本契約の未採用状態を入力条件として保持し、実行オーケストレーションの責任境界を別途定義する。
